\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq 1\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)

Proof.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\Leftrightarrow{}}\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq 1\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow(\text{By definition of }\mathsf{chompRec})

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\Leftrightarrow{}}\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq 1\implies

\displaystyle\hskip 63.0pt\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)

Proof by induction on
\tau
.

Case

\tau=\varnothing
::

\llbracket\varnothing\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\{\}
. As there are no heaps in the semantics, the case holds.

Case

\tau=\top
::

Let
h
be some heap from
\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=H
.
Let
h^{\prime}=h
except that
h^{\prime}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})[1\!:\!~]
.

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}_{1}
and
\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}
,
\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\top,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)=\top

and
\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}=H
.

We can conclude that
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\top,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

and
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
follows by construction of
h^{\prime}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})[1\!:\!~]
.

Case

\tau=\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
::

Let
h
be some heap from
\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We know
h=h1+\!\!+\ h2
, for some
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and some
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h1]}
.

We have to show that there exists some

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
.

We deconstruct
\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\Sigma y\!:\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}).\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},y,\mathsf{b_{0}})\ +

\displaystyle\phantom{{}=\llbracket{}}\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}.\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1).

\displaystyle\hskip 32.0pt\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\ +

\displaystyle\phantom{{}=\llbracket}\Sigma y\!:\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\},\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1).

\displaystyle\hskip 32.0pt\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1).

\displaystyle\hskip 32.0pt\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \cup

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}.\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}|h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}~\land

\displaystyle\hskip 52.0pth_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}))\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}~\cup

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}|h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}~\land

\displaystyle\hskip 52.0pth_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}

By case distinction on the length of
{pkt_{in}}
in
h_{1}
.

Subcase

\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert=0
::

By definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,

\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, because,
\tau_{1}
can’t contain a reference to the newly added bit in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)(\iota)
, from which follows that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By semantics of heap types,
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By IH there exists

h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}

such that
h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)
.

Let
h^{\prime}=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
.

We conclude that

\begin{split}h^{\prime}\in\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}|&h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}~\land\\
&h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}\end{split}

and thus

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By assumption
\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert=0
, we can conclude that

h_{1}+\!\!+\ \mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)

thus

\begin{split}h^{\prime}&=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}\\
&=h_{1}+\!\!+\ \mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)\\
&=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)\\
&=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)\end{split}

Subcase

\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert\neq 0
::

By IH there exists
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, such that

h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)
.

By Lemma
B.14
follows that

h_{2}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}

Let
h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
.

We conclude that

\begin{split}h^{\prime}\in\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}|&h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \land\\
&h_{2}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}))\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}\end{split}

and thus
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

With

\begin{split}h^{\prime}&=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}\\
&=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)+\!\!+\ h_{2}\\
&=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)\\
&=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)\end{split}

we can conclude this case.

Case

\tau=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}
::

Let
h
be some heap from
\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, we know
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
or
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

By IH we know that there exists

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
.

By set theory and

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}})+\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\ +

\displaystyle\hskip 14.5pt\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}~\cup

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

we conclude
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

Symmetric to previous subcase.

Case

\tau=\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\}
::

Let
h
be some heap from
\llbracket\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

By the semantics of heap types, we know that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h]}=\mathsf{true}
.
By induction hypothesis there exists
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
.

By
Lemma

B.13
we know that

\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h]}=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\varphi}_{1}(\varphi,y,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h^{\prime}]}

To apply
Lemma

B.13
, we must show that
x
is not free in
\varphi
, if
x\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{E})
.
If this does not hold, there is no
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, which violates our initial assumption.
With

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\{y:\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}})\mid\mathsf{chomp}^{\varphi}_{1}(\varphi,y,\mathsf{b_{0}})\},\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\{y:\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\mid

\displaystyle\hskip 32.0pt\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\varphi}_{1}(\varphi,y,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h|h\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \land

\displaystyle\hskip 38.5pt\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\varphi}_{1}(\varphi,y,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h]}\}

and with our assumptions from Lemma
B.13
and the induction hypothesis, we conclude that

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
.

Case

\tau=\tau_{1}{[}y\mapsto\tau_{2}{]}
::

Let
h
be some heap from
\llbracket\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We know that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}
for some
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

By IH there exists some
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}

such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
.

To conclude this case, we must show that

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

From
\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}
,
\mathsf{chomp}_{1}
and the semantics of heap types, we get:

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}})[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h_{11}|h_{22}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\land h_{11}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{22}]}\}

With
h_{11}=h^{\prime}
and
h_{22}=h_{2}
, we can conclude that

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

To argue that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, we make a case distinction on
x\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{E})
.

Subcase

x\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{E})
::

x
cannot appear free in
\tau
and thereby also not in
\tau_{2}
, otherwise there would be no
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, thus for all
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
,
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Subcase

x\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{E})
::

By assumption, there is some
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By semantics of heap types, all formulae in
\tau_{2}
referencing
x
, evaluate to true, i.e., they only refer to information contained in
\mathcal{E}(x)
.
By assumption,
\mathcal{E}(x)({pkt_{in}})=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)({pkt_{in}})
,
\mathcal{E}(x)(pkt_{out})=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)(pkt_{out})
, and

\mathcal{E}(x)(\iota)@h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)(\iota)
.
Since all information of
\mathcal{E}(x)
is preserved in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

∎

Lemma B.16 (ChompRec Unroll).

For all instances
\iota
, if
m+1\leq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
,
then

\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,x,\iota),1,x,\iota)=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,x,\iota)

Proof.

By induction on
n
.

Case

n=0
::

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,0,x,\iota),1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,x,\iota)

Case

n=1
::

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,x,\iota),1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},\iota,1),0,x,\iota),1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},\iota,1),1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,2,x,\iota)

Case

n=m
::

We assume that the lemma holds for
n=m
.
We now have to show that the lemma also holds for
n=m+1
.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,x,\iota),1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},\iota,m+1),m,x,\iota),

\displaystyle\hskip 58.0pt1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},\iota,m+1),m+1,x,\iota)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+2,x,\iota)

∎

Lemma B.17 (Semantic Chomp Unroll).

For all heaps
h
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, if
\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq n+1
, then
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n),1)=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n+1)

Proof.

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
,

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n),1)

\displaystyle=h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[n\!:\!\ ]][{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[n\!:\!\ ]]({pkt_{in}})[1\!:\!\ ]]

\displaystyle=h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[n\!:\!\ ]]({pkt_{in}})[1\!:\!\ ]]

\displaystyle=h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[n+1\!:\!\ ]]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n+1)

∎

Lemma B.18 (Chomp Slice).

For all heaps
h
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, if
\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq n+1
, then

\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n)({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]=h({pkt_{in}})[n\!:\!n+1]

Proof.

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
,

\phantom{{}={}}\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n)({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]=h[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[n\!:\!\ ]]({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
bv=h({pkt_{in}})=\langle b_{0},...,b_{n},...,b_{m}\rangle
.

Let
bv^{\prime}
be the bit vector we obtain after removing the first
n
bits from
bv^{\prime}
,
bv^{\prime}=\langle b_{n},...,b_{m}\rangle
.

Accessing the first bit of
bv^{\prime}
gives us bit
b_{n}
, which is also the n-th bit in
bv
, i.e.,
bv[n\!:\!n+1]
.
∎

Lemma B.19 (Semantic ChompRec).

Fix overflow

For all heap types
\tau
, environments
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, variables
x
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
if
x
does not appear free in
\tau
, and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!n]])]
,
then
\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq n\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,n,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n)
.

Proof.

Proof by induction on
n
.

Case

n=0
::

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\Leftrightarrow{}}\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq 0\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,0,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,0)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=h

Let
h^{\prime}=h
, i.e., we have to show that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
By assumption,
x
is not free in
\tau
, i.e., the binding of
x
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has no effect on the semantics of
\tau
.
Since
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
are otherwise identical,
\tau
evaluated in both environments is described by the same set of heaps, from which we can conclude that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Case

n=1
::

\phantom{{}\Leftrightarrow{}}\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq 1\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)

The result directly follows by
Lemma

B.15
.

Case

n=m+1
::

We assume that the lemma holds for
n=m
, i.e.,

\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq m\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)

We have to show that the lemma also holds for
n=m+1
, i.e.,

\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}.\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq m+1\implies\exists h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m+1)

where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!m+1],{pkt_{in}}\mapsto\langle\rangle,pkt_{out}\mapsto\langle\rangle]]
.

Let
h
be some heap
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}
.

By induction hypothesis, there exists some
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}}
, such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}_{0}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!m]])]
.
We use (A) to refer to this assumption.

By
Lemma

B.15
, for all
h_{1}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
, there exists some heap
h_{1}^{\prime}
such that
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x),1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}_{1}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{1}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]])]
, and
h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)
.

Since, by assumption,
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}}
and also
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}
, we can define
h_{1}
to be equal to
h^{\prime}
, i.e.,
h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h^{\prime},1)
.

From
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)
follows
h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m),1)
.

From
Lemma

B.17
also follows that
h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m+1)
.

We must show that
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

We know that

h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x),1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}}

and by
Lemma

B.16
,
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}}
, so we must show that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}
.

By assumption,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}_{1}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{1}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]])]
, where
h_{1}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)
(by IH).

Also by assumption,
\mathcal{E}(x)(\iota)=h({pkt_{in}})[0:m]
, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!m]@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]])]
.

Again, substituting
h_{1}
with
h^{\prime}
, and by
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)
, we obtain
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!m]@\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,m)({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]])]
.

By
Lemma

B.18
and by definition of bit vector concatenation,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!m+1]])]=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma B.20 (Semantic Chomp).

If
x
does not appear free in
\tau
, then forall heaps
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
where
\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
,
there exists
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
such that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h({pkt_{in}})[0:\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]])]
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}
, we know that

\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota),x,\iota)

The result follows from
Lemma

B.19
.
∎

Lemma B.21 (Semantic Chomp
1
Inverse).

For all
x
,
v
,
\tau
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}
such that

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
and
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and
x
not free in
\tau
and
\mathsf{sizeof}(v)\geq 1
,
there exists
h
and
\mathcal{E}
such that,

(1)

h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and

(2)

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)
and

and

(3)

\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x
and

(4)

v=h({pkt_{in}})[0:1]

or

(3)

x\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{E})
and

(4)

v=\mathcal{E}(x)(\iota)@h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]
and

(5)

\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0:\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

Proof.

We refer to the general assumptions as follows:

(A)

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])

(B)

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and

(C)

x
not free in
\tau
and

(D)

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)\geq 1

Proof by induction on
\tau
.
By definition of
\mathsf{chompRec}
follows that

\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}

Case

\tau=\varnothing
::

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\varnothing,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\varnothing,\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\varnothing\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{\}

As there is no
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, this case is immediate.

Case

\tau=\top
::

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\top,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\top,\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
be arbitrary.

Let
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\top,1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{H}
be arbitrary.
We have to distinguish two cases.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x
and let
h=h^{\prime}[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto v@h^{\prime}({pkt_{in}})]
, i.e.,
h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]=v
.

(1)
follows by the semantics of heap types.

(2)
follows by the definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
.

(3)
and
(4)
immediately follow from the definition of
h
and
\mathcal{E}
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0:\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]
and let
h=h^{\prime}[{pkt_{in}}\mapsto v[\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)]@h^{\prime}({pkt_{in}})]
.

(1)
follows by the semantics of heap types.

(2)
follows by the definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}

(3)
and
(4)
immediately follow from the definition of
h
and
\mathcal{E}

Case

\tau=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}
::

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
be arbitrary.

Let
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \cup

\displaystyle\hskip 10.5pt\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,x\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\cup\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{2},1,x\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

We have to distinguish two cases,

(1):

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and

(2):

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{2},1,x\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Subcase

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,x\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
::

We further distinguish between the size of
v
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A2):

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x

(A4):

v=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
h=h_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
follows from the semantics of heap types and by (A1).
The rest is immediate.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A2):

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

(A4):

v=\mathcal{E}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
h=h_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
follows from the semantics of heap types and by (A1).
The rest is immediate.

Subcase

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{2},1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
::

Symmetric to previous subcase.

Case

\tau=\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
::

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
be arbitrary.
Let
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\Sigma y\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}|h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 53.0pth_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}))\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}~\cup

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\{h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}|h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 53.0pth_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}\}

Case distinction on the membership of
h^{\prime}
.

Subcase

h^{\prime}\ \mathit{contained\ in\ the\ first\ subset}
::

(A1):

h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
and

(A2):

h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and

(A3):

h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chompRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},x,\mathsf{b_{0}}))\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}
.

We distinguish two additional cases.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A4):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A5):

h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A6):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x

(A7):

v=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

By
Lemma

B.14
,
h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Since
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
is defined, i.e., they have disjoint sets of headers and
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
does not affect the validity of headers,
h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
is defined.

Let
h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

(1)
follows by
(A4)
and
h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
. The latter holds, because
x
is not free in
\tau_{2}
by assumption.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime},1)\Leftrightarrow\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime},1)
.
This equality holds, because chomping of one bit from the input packet of
h_{1}
and then concatenating
h_{2}^{\prime}
yields the same heap as concatenating both heaps and then removing the first bit of the input packet.

(3)
follows by
(A6)
and
(4)
follows by
(A7)
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A8):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A9):

h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A10):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

(A11):

v=\mathcal{E}_{1}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

By
Lemma

B.14
,
h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Since
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
is defined, i.e., they have disjoint sets of headers and
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
does not affect the validity of headers,
h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
is defined.

Let
h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

(1)
follows by
(A8)
and
h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
. The latter holds, because
x
is not free in
\tau_{2}
by assumption.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime},1)\Leftrightarrow\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime},1)
.
This equality holds, because chomping of one bit from the input packet of
h_{1}
and then concatenating
h_{2}^{\prime}
yields the same heap as concatenating both heaps and then removing the first bit of the input packet.

(3)
follows by
(A10)
and
(4)
follows by
(A11)
and
(5)
follows by
(A10)
.

Subcase

h^{\prime}\ \mathit{contained\ in\ the\ second\ subset}
::

(A1):

h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}
and

(A2):

h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and

(A3):

h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{2},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]}
.

We distinguish two cases.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

By IH, for every
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, there exists
h_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, such that

(A4):

h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}

(A5):

h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)

(A6):

\mathcal{E}_{2}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime}]\setminus x

(A7):

v=h_{2}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
h=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{2}\setminus y
.
We have to show that
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By assumption,
x
is not free in
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
.
By
(A2)
and by the fact that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x
,
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and by subtyping,
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

(1)
follows together with
(A4)
.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)\Leftrightarrow h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ \mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)
.
Since by
(A2)
, the input packet of
h_{1}^{\prime}
is empty, the input packet of both heaps are equal.

(3)
follows by
(A6)
and
(4)
follows by
(A7)
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

By IH, for every
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, there exists
h_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, such that

(A8):

h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}

(A9):

h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)

(A10):

\mathcal{E}_{2}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}^{\prime},x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

(A11):

v=\mathcal{E}_{2}(x)(\iota)@h_{2}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
h=h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{2}\setminus y
.
To show that
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By assumption that
x
is not free in
\tau_{1}
and by
(A2)
,
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert=0\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and by subtyping,
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

(1)
follows together with
(A8)
.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)\Leftrightarrow h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ \mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{2},1)=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}^{\prime}+\!\!+\ h_{2},1)
.
Since by
(A2)
, the input packet of
h_{1}^{\prime}
is empty, the input packet of both heaps are equal.

(3)
follows by
(A10)
and
(4)
follows by
(A11)
and
(5)
follows by
(A10)
.

Case

\tau=\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\}
::

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
be arbitrary.

Let
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h^{\prime}|h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\varphi}_{1}(\varphi,y,\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h^{\prime}]}\}

We distinguish two cases.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A2):

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x

(A4):

v=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
h=h_{1}
.
To show
(1)
, we must show that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
, which follows by
(A1)
and
Lemma

B.13
.

(2)
follows by
(A2)
,
(3)
follows by
(A3)
and
(4)
follows by
(A4)
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

By IH, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A2):

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

(A4):

v=\mathcal{E}_{1}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
h=h_{1}
.
To show
(1)
, we must show
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
, which follows by
(A1)
and
Lemma

B.13
.

(2)
follows by
(A2)
,
(3)
follows by
(A3)
,
(4)
follows by
(A4)
and
(5)
follows by
(A3)
.

Case

\tau=\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]
::

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
be arbitrary.

Let
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}})[y\mapsto\tau_{2}],\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)[y\mapsto\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\tau_{2},\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{heapRef}_{1}(\mathsf{chomp}_{1}(\tau_{1},\mathsf{b_{0}}),\mathsf{b_{0}},x,\iota,1)[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\{h_{1}^{\prime}|h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\wedge h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau_{1},1,x,\iota)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}\}

We distinguish two cases.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=1
::

By IH, for every
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}

(A2):

h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{2}]\setminus x

(A4):

v=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
h=h_{1}
.
Since
x
and
y
not free in
\tau_{2}
, for every heap
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
also holds that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
,
from which we can conclude
(1)
, i.e.,
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,1)=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)
, which follows from
(A2)
and the fact that
h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}
.

(3)
follows by choice of
\mathcal{E}
and
(A3)
.

(4)
follows by
(A4)
.

Subcase

\mathsf{sizeof}(v)>1
::

By IH, for every
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, there exists
h_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that

(A1):

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}

(A2):

h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)

(A3):

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{2},x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(v)-1]])]

(A4):

v=\mathcal{E}_{1}(x)(\iota)@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!1]

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
h=h_{1}
.

To show
(1)
, we must show that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
.
Since
x
and
y
not free in
\tau_{2}
, for every heap
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
also holds that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
.
The result follows by the semantics of heap types.

To show
(2)
, we must show that
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},1)
, which follows from
(A2)
and
h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}
.

(3)
follows by
(A3)
,
(4)
follows by
(A4)
and
(5)
follows also by
(A3)
.

∎

Lemma B.22 (Semantic ChompRec Inverse).

For all variables
x
, values
v
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, heap types
\tau
, environments
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and heaps
h^{\prime}
such that

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
and
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,n,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and
x
not free in
\tau
and
\mathsf{sizeof}(v)=n
,
there exists
h
and
\mathcal{E}
such that

h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x
and
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,n)=h^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Proof by induction on
n
.

Case

n=0
::

By definition of
\mathsf{chompRec}
,
\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,0,\iota,x)=\tau
.

Together with assumption
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,n,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, we know that
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Let
h=h^{\prime}
.
By assumption,
x
is not free in
\tau
, thus the binding of
x
in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
does not affect the semantics of
\tau
.
We can therefore remove the binding altogether, so
\tau
describes the same set of heaps both in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and in
\mathcal{E}
.
We can conclude that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,0)=h^{\prime}
follows from the definition of semantic chomp.

Case

n=1
::

The result directly follows by
Lemma

B.21
.

Case

n=m+1
::

We assume that the lemma holds for
n=m
.
We have to show that the lemma also holds for
n=m+1
.
Let
h_{0}^{\prime}
be some heap such that
h_{0}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m+1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}}
.
Together with
Lemma

B.16
, we can conclude that

h_{0}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x),1,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}}

By
Lemma

B.21
, there is some
h_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}
such that
h_{1}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,m,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}}
and
h_{0}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1}^{\prime},1)

where
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v[0\!:\!m]])]
.
By IH,
there exists a
h_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, such that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
where
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\prime}\setminus x
and
h_{1}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},m)
.
From
h_{0}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},m),1)
and
Lemma

B.17
, follows

h_{0}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h_{1},m+1)
.

∎

Lemma B.23 (Semantic Chomp Inverse).

For all variables
x
, values
v
, instances
\iota
, heap types
\tau
, environments
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and heaps
h^{\prime}
such that

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(x)=(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])
and
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and
x
not free in
\tau
,
there exists
h
and
\mathcal{E}
such that

h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\setminus x
and
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))=h^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}
, we know that

\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)=\mathsf{chompRec}(\tau,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota),x,\iota)

The result follows by
Lemma

B.22
.
∎

Lemma B.24 (Formulae Preservation).

If
\Gamma;\tau\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
and

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau
and

\langle I,O,H,\varphi\rangle\rightarrow\varphi^{\prime}

then
\Gamma;\tau\vdash\varphi^{\prime}:\mathbb{B}
.

Proof.

By induction on a derivation of
\Gamma;\tau\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
.
∎

Lemma B.25 (Semantic Formulae Preservation).

If
\Gamma;\tau\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
and

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau
and

\langle I,O,H,\varphi\rangle\rightarrow\varphi^{\prime}

then

\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}=\llbracket\varphi^{\prime}[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

Proof.

By induction on a derivation of
\Gamma;\tau\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
.
∎

Lemma B.26 (Expression Preservation).

If
\Gamma;\tau\vdash e:\sigma
and

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau
and

\langle I,O,H,e\rangle\rightarrow e^{\prime}

then
\Gamma;\tau\vdash e^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Proof.

By induction on a typing derivation of
\Gamma;\tau\vdash e:\sigma
.
∎

Lemma B.27 (Semantic Expression Preservation).

If
\Gamma;\tau\vdash e:\sigma
and

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau
and

\langle I,O,H,e\rangle\rightarrow e^{\prime}

then

\llbracket e[x/\ cmdVar]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}=\llbracket e^{\prime}[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

Proof.

By induction on a typing derivation of
\Gamma;\tau\vdash e:\sigma
.
∎

Theorem B.28 (Preservation).

If
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}
,

\langle I,O,H,c\rangle\rightarrow\langle I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle
,

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau_{1}
, then

\exists\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime},x^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime}.\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c^{\prime}:(x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and

(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})\models_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\tau_{1}^{\prime}
and

\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

Proof.

Directly follows by the slightly stronger
Lemma

B.29
.
∎

Lemma B.29 (Preservation AUX).

If
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}
,

\langle I,O,H,c\rangle\rightarrow\langle I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle
,

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau_{1}
, then

\exists\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime},x^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime}.\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c^{\prime}:(x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and

(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})\models_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\tau_{1}^{\prime}
and

\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

Proof.

By induction on a derivation of
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}
with case analysis on the last rule used.
We refer to the proof goals as follows:

(1)

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c^{\prime}:(x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}

(2)

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma^{\prime}

(3)

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}

(4)

\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

(5)

(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})\models_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\tau_{1}^{\prime}

(6)

\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

General assumptions:

(A)

\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}

(B)

\langle I,O,H,c\rangle\rightarrow\langle I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle

(C)

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma

(D)

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}}\tau_{1}

Case

T-Add
::

By inversion of rule
T-Add
, we get

(A1):

c=\mathit{add}{\iota}

(A2):

\mathsf{Excludes}~\Gamma~\tau_{1}~\iota

(A3):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}

Only evaluation rule
E-Add
applies to
c
:

(A4):

\iota\not\in\mathit{dom}(H)

(A5):

\mathcal{HT}(\iota)=\eta

(A6):

\mathit{init}=v
.

(A7):

I^{\prime}=I
and
O^{\prime}=O
and
H^{\prime}=H[\iota\mapsto v]
and
c^{\prime}=\mathit{skip}

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]
.

Let
\tau_{1}^{\prime}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}
and

\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\{w:\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}\mid w\equiv x^{\prime}\}
.

(1)
follows by
T-Skip
and
(2)
follows by assumptions
(C)
and
(D)
and
Lemma

B.4
.

(3)
and
(4)
are immediate.

To show
(5)
, we must show that
(I,O,H[\iota\mapsto v])\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}
.
By
Ent-Sigma
, we must show that

(5.1):

(I,O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}
and

(5.2):

(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)y\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}
.

(5.1)
follows by
Ent-Refine
and
(D)
.
To show
(5.2)
, by
Ent-Refine
, we must show that

(5.2.1):

(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])\models_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\{z:\top\mid z.\iota.\mathit{valid}\wedge\bigwedge_{\kappa\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})}\neg\kappa.\mathit{valid}\}
and

(5.2.2):

\llbracket z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto(I,O,H),z\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])]}=\mathit{true}

(5.2.1)
follows by
Ent-Refine
,
Ent-Top
and the semantics of formulae.

(5.2.2)
follows from the semantics of formulae.

(6)
follows by

\displaystyle\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(I,O,H[\iota\mapsto v])\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h\mid h\in\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 141.5ptz.\iota=v\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I,O,H[\iota\mapsto v])]}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 33.0pt\llbracket w\equiv x^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I,O,H[\iota\mapsto v]),w\mapsto h]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\{w:\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}\mid

\displaystyle\hskip 23.0ptw\equiv x^{\prime}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I,O,H[\iota\mapsto v])]}

Case

T-Ascribe
::

By inversion of rule
T-Ascribe
, we get

(A1):

c=c_{a}\ \mathit{as}\ (x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash c_{a}:(x:\tau_{1})\rightarrow\tau_{2}

There is one evaluation rule that applies to
c
,
E-Ascribe
, so

I^{\prime}=I
and
O^{\prime}=O
and
H^{\prime}=H
and
c^{\prime}=c_{a}
.
Let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma
,
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}
,
\tau_{1}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
.

(1)
follows by
(A2)
,
(2)
follows by assumption
(C)
.

(3)
and
(4)
are immediate.

(5)
follows by assumption
(D)
and
(6)
follows from the equality of
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
, which itself follows by reflexivity.

Case

T-Extract
::

By inversion of rule
T-Extract
, we get

(A1):

c=\mathit{extract(\iota)}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{pkt_{in}}(\tau_{1})=\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)

(A3):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid\\
y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}

Only evaluation rule
E-Extract
applies to
c
:

(A4):

\mathit{deserialize_{\mathcal{HT}(\iota)}}(I)=(v,I^{\prime})

(A5):

O^{\prime}=O

(A6):

H^{\prime}=H[\iota\mapsto v]

(A7):

c^{\prime}=\mathit{skip}

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma,x:\tau_{1},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]
.

Let
\tau_{1}^{\prime}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}
.

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\{v:\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\mid v\equiv x\}
.

(1)
follows by
T-Skip
and
(2)
follows by assumptions
(C)
and
(D)
and
Lemma

B.4
.

(3)
and
(4)
are immediate.

To show
(5)
, we must show that
(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v])\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}
.

By
Ent-Sigma
, we must show that

(5.1):

(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}
, which follows by
Ent-Refine
and the semantics of types and

(5.2):

(I^{\prime},O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])]}\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}

By
Ent-Refine
, we must show that

(5.2.1):

(I^{\prime},O,H)\models_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])]}\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)

By
Lemma

B.2
, it is sufficient to show that

(I^{\prime},O,H)\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])]}

By assumption (D) and by
Lemma

B.2
follows
(I,O,H)\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

By
Lemma

B.20
, there exists some heap

h\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v])]}

such that
h=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}((I,O,H),\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
.

From the definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
follows that

\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}((I,O,H),\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))=(I^{\prime\prime},O,H)

where
I^{\prime\prime}=I[\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\!:\!\ ]=I^{\prime}
.

(5.2.2):

\llbracket y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge\ z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge\ \\
z\equiv_{\iota}x\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto v]),z\mapsto(I^{\prime},O,H)]}
, which follows by the definition of
deserialize
and the semantics of formulae and expressions.

Finally, we must show that
\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v])]}
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.

\displaystyle\hskip 23.0pt\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 109.0ptz\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}\mid h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pth_{2}\in\llbracket\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 109.0ptz.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{1}]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}\mid h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pth_{2}\in\{h_{2}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{1}]}\wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 90.0pt\llbracket y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 95.0ptz\equiv_{\iota}x\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{1},z\mapsto h_{2}^{\prime}]}\}\}

By
Lemma

B.20

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}\mid h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 45.0pth_{2}\in\{h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\wedge h_{2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 86.0pt\llbracket y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 91.0ptz\equiv_{\iota}x\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{1},z\mapsto h_{2}^{\prime}]}\}\}

By definition of
\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}
and semantics of types

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v])\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h\mid h\in\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.

\displaystyle\hskip 55.0pt\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0ptz.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v])]}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 35.0pt\llbracket v\equiv x^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v]),v\mapsto h]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\{v:\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.

\displaystyle\hskip 38.5pt\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\hskip 125.0ptz.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\mid

\displaystyle\hskip 23.0ptv\equiv x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O,H[\iota\mapsto v])]}

(6)
and concludes the case.

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O.H)]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{22}|h_{12}\in\llbracket\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\wedge h_{22}\in\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{12}]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{22}|h_{12}=(I,O,H)\wedge h_{22}\in\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h_{12}]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h_{22}|h_{22}\in\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(I,O,H)]}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

This shows
(6)
and concludes this subcase.

Subcase

E-Seq1
::

(A7):

c^{\prime}=c_{1}^{\prime};c_{2}

(A8):

\langle I,O,H,c_{1}\rangle\rightarrow\langle I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime}\rangle

By IH with
(A2)
,
(A7)
,
(C)
and
(D)
, there exists
\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{12}^{\prime},x^{\prime}
, such that,

(A9):

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}^{\prime}:(x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}
where

(A10):

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma^{\prime}

(A11):

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}

(A12):

\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

(A13):

(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})\models_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\tau_{1}^{\prime}

(A14):

\llbracket\tau_{12}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{12}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

(1)
follows by
T-Seq
, if we can show that there exists some
\tau_{22}^{\prime}
, such that

\Gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}
where

\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{22}^{\prime}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}^{\prime}]
:

\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}^{\prime}:(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}\quad\Gamma^{\prime},x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}^{\prime};c_{2}:(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}^{\prime}]}\quad\textsc{{T-Seq} }

By
B.11
with
(A3)
and
(A13)
, there exists some
\tau_{22}^{\prime}
such that

(A15):

\Gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}

(A16):

\forall h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{12}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}.\llbracket\tau_{22}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime}),y\mapsto h^{\prime}]}\subseteq\\
\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h^{\prime}]}

(2)
follows by
(A10)
and
(3)
follows by
(A11)
,
(4)
follows by
(A12)
and
(5)
follows by
(A13)
.

(6)
follows by

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x^{\prime}\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\llbracket\tau_{22}^{\prime}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}\subseteq\llbracket\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bigcup_{h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\tau_{12}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime})]}}\llbracket\tau_{22}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto(I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime}),y\mapsto h^{\prime}]}\ \subseteq

\displaystyle\bigcup_{h\in\llbracket\tau_{12}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H)]}}\llbracket\tau_{22}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(I,O,H),y\mapsto h]}

and by
(A14)
,
(A16)
and the semantics of heap types.

Case

::

Immediately holds as there is no
c^{\prime}
such that
\langle I,O,H,c\rangle\rightarrow\langle I^{\prime},O^{\prime},H^{\prime},c^{\prime}\rangle
.

::

∎
