Figure 20
.
Algorithmic typing rules for
\Pi
4. Assume binders are fresh in all rules

Lemma C.1 (Subtype Reflexivity).

For all subtyping contexts
\Gamma
and heap types
\tau
,
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\tau
.

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

Lemma C.2 (Subtype Transitivity).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{2}
, and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}<:\tau_{3}
,
then
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{3}
.

Proof.

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{2}
and also assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}<:\tau_{3}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
be arbitrary.
By the first assumption
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the second assumption
h\in\llbracket\tau_{3}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
∎

Lemma C.3 (Environment Entails Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{3}
, and

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{3}
, then

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{3}
and
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{3})
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
, such that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma
, and

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
The definition of subtyping gives
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{3}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
The result follows by definition of entailment.
∎

Lemma C.4 (Context Strengthening).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{3}
and

\Gamma,x:\tau_{3}\vdash\tau_{2}<:\tau_{4}

then

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}<:\tau_{4}
.

Proof.

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{3}
and further assume
\Gamma,x:\tau_{3}\vdash\tau_{2}<:\tau_{4}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
.
By Lemma
C.3
and
the first assumption,
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{3})
. Let

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, the second assumption gives that

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, and we’re done.
∎

Lemma C.5 (Packet Bound Subtype).

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{{pkt_{in}}}(\tau)\geq N
iff
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}
.

Proof.

We show each direction separately.

(
\Rightarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{{pkt_{in}}}(\tau)\geq N
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
be arbitrary.
By definition,
\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert\geq N
.
By the definition of subtyping, it suffices to show
h\in\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq N\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition,
\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq N\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\{h\mid h\in\mathcal{H}\wedge h({pkt_{in}})\geq N\}
, which concludes this case.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq N\}
.
We have to show that
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{{pkt_{in}}}(\tau)\geq N
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
be arbitrary.
By the definition of subtyping,
h\in\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq N\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition of the semantics, we can conclude
h({pkt_{in}})\geq N
.

∎

Lemma C.6 (Chomp Subtype).

If
x
not free in
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
,
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{{pkt_{in}}}(\tau)\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)

and
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\tau^{\prime}
,
then
\Gamma,x:\{y:\iota\mid y.{pkt_{in}}=y.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\vdash\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)<:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau^{\prime},\iota,x)
.

Proof.

Given some heap
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau^{\prime},\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
By
Lemma

B.23
there exists some
\mathcal{E}
and
h^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket\tau^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
,
such that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h^{\prime\prime}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]])]
and

h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
.
By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\tau^{\prime}
, we also know that
h^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

By
Lemma

B.20
, we know that there exists
h\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}}
such that

h=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto(\langle\rangle,\langle\rangle,[\iota\mapsto h^{\prime\prime}({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]])]
.
From
h^{\prime}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
and
h=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
follows by the transitivity of equality that
h^{\prime}=h
.
By the fact that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
follows that for every heap
h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau^{\prime},\iota,x)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
also holds that

h^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}}
.
∎

Lemma C.7 (Refinement Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}<:\tau
,
then

\Gamma\vdash\{x:\tau^{\prime}\mid\varphi\}<:\{x:\tau\mid\varphi\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\vdash\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\{x:\tau^{\prime}\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then
h\in\llbracket\tau^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, and
\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h]}=\mathit{true}
.
By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}<:\tau
,
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Conclude
h\in\llbracket\{x:\tau\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
by definition.
The result follows.
∎

Lemma C.8 (Sigma Left-Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}

then

\Gamma\vdash\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}^{\prime}.\tau_{2}<:\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
, and
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}^{\prime}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition of the semantics,
h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
,where

h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h]}
.
By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
follows,
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the definition of the semantics,

h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
The result follows.
∎

Lemma C.9 (Sigma Right-Subtype).

If
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}

then

\Gamma\vdash\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition,

h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
such that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
,
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Notice that
\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})
, so by assumption
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
follows
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
By the definition of the semantics

h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
. The result follows.
∎

Lemma C.10 (Substitution Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
and

\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
, then

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}[x\mapsto\tau_{1}^{\prime}]<:\tau_{2}[x\mapsto\tau_{1}]
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}[x\mapsto\tau_{1}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then we know
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
tells us that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Notice that
\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})
.
Assumption
\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
gives
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
By the definition of the semantics of heap types,

h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}[x\mapsto\tau_{1}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
∎

Lemma C.11 (Choice Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
,
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
,
then

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
. Let
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}^{\prime}+\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By semantics of heap types, either
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
or
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
it also holds that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and we can conclude that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
it also holds that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and we can conclude that
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

∎

Lemma C.12 (Context-Bound Refinement Subtype).

If
\mathsf{heap}
is the only free binder in
\varphi
,
and
\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}

then

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{2}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}<:\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
.
We can write this as
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
, where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{2}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.
Compute as follows, recalling that
\mathsf{heap}
is the only free binder in
\varphi
:

\displaystyle\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}

Together with assumption
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, we get
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, and thus

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]\models\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}
.

With assumption
\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
, we can conclude that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Since we already have that
\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
, it follows that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, which is what we wanted to show.
∎

Lemma C.13 (If Choice Subtype).

If
\mathsf{heap}
is the only free binder in
\varphi
and

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{12}^{\prime}<:\tau_{12}
, and

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}<:\tau_{22}
,
then

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\{y:\tau_{12}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}<:\{y:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

Proof.

By Lemmas
C.12
and
C.11
.
∎

Lemma C.14 (Algorithmic Weakening).

If
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}
and variable
y
does not appear free in
\tau_{1}
or
\tau_{2}
,
then
\Gamma,y:\tau\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}
for any heap type
\tau
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation.
∎

Lemma C.15 (Typing Context Subtype).

If
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}
and

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
,
then
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}
.

Proof.

If
x
is not free in
\tau_{12}
or
\tau_{22}
, the result follows from
Lemma

C.14
.
Otherwise we proceed by induction on the typing derivation.
We refer to the general assumptions as follows:

(A)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(B)

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}

Case

T-Extract-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Extract-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\mathit{extract(\iota)}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A2):

\tau_{22}=\Sigma z\!:\{v:\iota\mid\varphi_{1}\}.\{v:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{12},\iota,z)\mid\varphi_{2}\}

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\{z:\top\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A3)
and
(B)
follows

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\{z:\top\mid\lvert z.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}

The result follows by
T-Extract-Algo
.

Case

T-Seq-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Seq-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{1};c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1},y:\tau_{12}\vdash c_{2}:(z:\tau_{12}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}

(A4):

\tau_{22}=\tau_{22}^{\prime}[z\mapsto\tau_{12}^{\prime}]

By IH applied to
(A2)
and
(B)
follows

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}

By IH applied to
(A3)
and
(B)
follows

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime},y:\tau_{12}\vdash c_{2}:(z:\tau_{12}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}

The result follows by
T-Seq-Algo
with
(A5)
and
(A6)
.

Case

T-Skip-Algo
::

The result immediately follows by
T-Skip-Algo
.

Case

T-Remit-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Remit-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\iota_{\approx}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A1)
and
(B)
follows

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\iota_{\approx}

The result follows by
T-Remit-Algo
.

Case

T-Reset-Algo
::

The result immediately follows by
T-Reset-Algo
.

Case

T-Ascribe-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Ascribe-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{0}\ as\ (y:\hat{\tau}_{12})\to\tau_{22}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{0}:(y:\hat{\tau}_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\hat{\tau}_{12}

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1},y:\hat{\tau}_{12}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}<:\tau_{22}

By IH applied to
(A2)
and
(B)
follows

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{0}:(y:\hat{\tau}_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A3)
and
(B)
follows

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\hat{\tau}_{12}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A4)
and
(B)
follows

(A7):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime},y:\hat{\tau}_{12}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}<:\tau_{22}

The result follows by
T-Ascribe-Algo
with
(A5)
,
(A6)
and
(A7)
.

Case

T-If-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-If-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\{z:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[z/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\{z:\tau_{12}\mid\neg\varphi[z/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime\prime}

(A3):

\tau_{22}=\{z:\tau_{12}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{z:\tau_{12}^{\prime\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1};\tau_{12}\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}

By IH applied to
(A1)
and
(B)
follows

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\{z:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[z/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}

By IH applied to
(A2)
and
(B)
follows

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{1}:(y:\{z:\tau_{12}\mid\neg\varphi[z/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime\prime}

Since
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1};\tau_{12}\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
, it also holds that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime};\tau_{12}\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}
.
The result then follows by
T-If-Algo
.

Case

T-Mod-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Mod-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\iota_{\approx}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1};\tau_{12}\vdash e:\mathsf{BV}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A1)
and
(B)
follows

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\iota_{\approx}

Since
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1};\tau_{12}\vdash e:\mathsf{BV}
, it also holds that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime};\tau_{12}\vdash e:\mathsf{BV}
.
The result follows by
T-Mod-Algo
.

Case

T-Add-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Add-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}

By
Lemma

C.4
applied to
(A1)
and
(B)
follows

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}

The result follows by

.

∎

Lemma C.16 (Algorithmic Input Subtype).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}
such that
x
is not free in
\tau_{1}
or
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
,
then there exists
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation.
We refer to the general assumptions as follows:

(A)

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}

(B)

\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}

(C)

x
not free in
\tau_{1}
or
\tau_{1}^{\prime}

We refer to the proof goals as follows:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}

(2)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}

Case

T-Extract-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Extract-Algo
, we know

(A1):

c=\mathit{extract(\iota)}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}

(A3):

\varphi_{1}\triangleq z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle

(A4):

\varphi_{2}\triangleq y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x

(A5):

\tau_{21}\triangleq\{z:\iota\mid\varphi_{1}\}

(A6):

\tau_{22}\triangleq\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid\varphi_{2}\}

(A7):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\tau_{21}.\tau_{22}

By
Lemma

C.2
with
(A)
and
(A2)
,

(A8):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}

By
Lemma

C.6
with
(A2)
and
(A)
,

(A9):

\Gamma,y:\tau_{21}\vdash\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1}^{\prime},\iota,y)<:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)

(1) follows by
T-Extract-Algo
.
(2) follows by Lemmas
C.7
and
C.9
and
(A9)
.

Case

T-Seq-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Seq-Algo
, we know

(A1):

c=c_{1};c_{2}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A4):

\tau_{2}=\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}]

By IH applied to
(A2)
and
(A)
, there is some
\tau_{12}^{\prime}
such that

(A5):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}
, and

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{12}^{\prime}<:\tau_{12}

By
Lemma

C.15
with
(A)
and
(A3)
,

(A7):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

By IH applied to
(A7)
and
(A6)
, there is some
\tau_{22}^{\prime}
such that

(A8):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}
, and

(A9):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime},y:\tau_{12}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}<:\tau_{22}

(1) follows by
T-Seq-Algo
with
(A5)
and
(A8)
.
To show (2), we just need to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}^{\prime}]<:\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}]
.
This follows by
Lemma

C.10
applied to
(A6)
and
(A9)
.

Case

T-Skip-Algo
::

Immediate by
T-Skip-Algo
.

Case

T-Remit-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Remit-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\iota_{\approx}

(A2):

c=\mathit{remit(\iota)}

(A3):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\epsilon\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.\iota\}

By
Lemma

C.2
with
(A1)
and
(A)
follows

(A4):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\iota_{\approx}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\epsilon\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.\iota\}
.
(1) follows by
T-Remit-Algo
.
By Lemma
C.4
, and since
x
does not occur free in
\tau_{1}
or
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
, we know

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}

By Lemma
C.7
we know

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\{z:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid z\equiv x\}<:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}

By
Lemma

C.8
with
\tau_{22}=\{z:\epsilon\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.\iota\}
follows

(A7):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid z\equiv x\}.\tau_{22}<:\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\tau_{22}

This shows (2) and concludes this case.

Case

T-Reset-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Reset-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\epsilon\mid z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.{pkt_{in}}=x.pkt_{out}\}.\{z:\epsilon\mid z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.{pkt_{in}}=z.{pkt_{in}}\}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
.
(1) follows by
T-Reset-Algo
and (2) follows by
Lemma

C.1
.

Case

T-Ascribe-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Ascribe-Algo
, we know

(A1):

c=c_{0}\ \mathit{as}\ (x:\hat{\tau}_{1})\to\tau_{2}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash c_{0}:(x:\hat{\tau}_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{c}

(A3):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\hat{\tau}_{1}

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\hat{\tau}_{1}\vdash\tau_{c}<:\tau_{2}

By
Lemma

C.2
applied to
(A)
and
(A3)
follows that

(A5):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\hat{\tau}_{1}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
.
(1) follows by
T-Ascribe-Algo
with
(A2)
,
(A4)
and
(A5)
.
(2) follows by
Lemma

C.1
.

Case

T-If-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-If-Algo
, we know

(A1):

c=\mathit{if(\varphi)\ then\ c_{1}\ else\ c_{2}}

(A2):

\Gamma;\tau_{1}\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}

(A3):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}

(A4):

\Gamma\vdash c_{2}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A5):

\tau_{2}=\{y:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

By
(A)
and
Lemma

C.7
, we know

(A6):

\Gamma\vdash\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}<:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}
, and

(A7):

\Gamma\vdash\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}<:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}

By applying the IH to
(A6)
and
(A3)
we get
\tau_{12}^{\prime}
such that

(A8):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}^{\prime}
, and

(A9):

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{12}^{\prime}<:\tau_{12}

By applying the IH to
(A7)
and
(A4)
we get
\tau_{22}^{\prime}
such that

(A10):

\Gamma\vdash c_{2}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}^{\prime}
, and

(A11):

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{22}^{\prime}<:\tau_{22}

From
(A2)
and
(A)
, we can conclude that

(A12):

\Gamma;\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\varphi:\mathbb{B}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\{y:\tau_{12}^{\prime}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}^{\prime}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}
.
(2) follows by
Lemma

C.13
.
(1) follows by
T-If-Algo
with
(A8)
,
(A10)
and
(A12)
.

Case

T-Mod-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Mod-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\iota_{\approx}

(A2):

\Gamma;\tau_{1}\vdash e:\mathsf{BV}

(A3):

\tau_{2}=\{y:\top\mid\varphi\wedge\varphi_{\iota}\wedge\varphi_{f}\wedge y.\iota.f=e[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

By
Lemma

C.2
with
(A)
and
(A1)
follows

(A4):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\iota_{\approx}

From
(A2)
and
(A)
, we can conclude

(A5):

\Gamma;\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash e:\mathsf{BV}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
.
(1) follows by
T-Mod-Algo
with
(A4)
and
(A5)
.
(2) follows by
Lemma

C.1
.

Case

T-Add-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Add-Algo
, we know

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}

(A2):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}

By
Lemma

C.2
with
(A1)
and
(A)
follows

(A3):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}

Let
\tau_{2}^{\prime}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}
.
(1) follows by
T-Add-Algo
.
By
Lemma

C.4
with
(A)
and
(C)
,

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{1}^{\prime}<:\tau_{1}

By
Lemma

C.7
follows

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\vdash\{z:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\mid z\equiv x\}<:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}

(2) follows by
Lemma

C.8
with
(A5)
.

∎

Lemma C.17 (Includes Subtype).

\mathsf{Includes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota\iff\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\iota_{\sim}

Proof.

Prove each direction separately

(
\Rightarrow
)

Assume
\mathsf{Includes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota
. Let

\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
. By
definition of the inclusion relation,
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(h)
. By the
definition of subtyping, it suffices to show

h\in\llbracket\iota_{\sim}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
. By definition,

\llbracket\iota_{\sim}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\{h\mid\iota\in\mathit{dom}(h)\}
, and
we’re done.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\iota_{\sim}
. Show

\mathsf{Includes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota
. To that end, let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and

h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
be arbitrary.
By the definition of subtyping,
h\in\llbracket\iota_{\sim}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition of the semantics, conclude
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(h)
.

∎

Lemma C.18 (Excludes Subtype).

\mathsf{Excludes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota
iff
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}

Proof.

Prove each direction separately

(
\Rightarrow
)

Assume
\mathsf{Excludes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition of the exclusion relation,
\iota\not\in\mathit{dom}(h)
.
By the definition of subtyping, it suffices to show
h\in\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition,
\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\{h\mid\iota\not\in\mathit{dom}(h)\}
, and we’re done.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}
.
Show
\mathsf{Excludes}~\Gamma~\tau~\iota
.
To that end, let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
be arbitrary.
By the definition of subtyping,
h\in\llbracket\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By definition of the semantics, conclude
\iota\not\in\mathit{dom}(h)
.

∎

Theorem C.19 (Algorithmic Typing Correctness).

For all subtyping contexts
\Gamma
, commands
c
, variables

, heap types
\tau_{1}
, and
\tau_{2}
,
where

is not free in
\tau_{1}

\Gamma\vdash c\!:\!(x:\tau_{1})\to\tau_{2}

if and only if

\tau_{2}^{\prime}
such that

\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
,
and

\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
.

(A3):

(A5):

which shows

.

0(2)
follows by

C.1

Appendix D
MTU Bound

We want to prove a theorem that says that if we have an maximum transmission unit (MTU)
N
for a packet,
then we can bound the number of bits in the types.

We want to prove that if a program typechecks with a bound on its input type,
then we can compute that maximum number of bits that we need to encode the input
type. Ideally, this would be the same bound; however, it is possible for the
text of the program to emit more bits from the incoming packet than is
allowed by the MTU. So we define a helper function
\mathsf{emit}(c)\in\mathbb{N}
that
over-approximates the maximum number of bits that could be emitted along
any program path in
c
. This is defined in Figure
21

\begin{array}[]{l>{\triangleq}cl}\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{extract(\iota)})&&0\\
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{if(b)\ then\ c_{1}\ else\ c_{2}})&&\max(\mathsf{emit}(c_{1}),\mathsf{emit}(c_{2}))\\
\mathsf{emit}(c_{1};c_{2})&&\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})\\
\mathsf{emit}(\iota.f:=t)&&0\\
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{remit(\iota)})&&\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\\
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{skip})&&0\\
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{reset})&&0\\
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{add}(\iota))&&0\\
\mathsf{emit}(c\ \mathit{as}\ \sigma)&&\mathsf{emit}(c)\end{array}

Figure 21
.
\mathsf{emit}(c)\in\mathbb{N}
computes the maximum number of bits that can be
emitted along any path in c.

We also need a way to state that a type
\tau
satisfies a given MTU
n
; that is
that for every denoted heap
h
,
{pkt_{in}}
and
pkt_{out}
use fewer than
n
bits
combined. This is defined formally in Figure
22
.

\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq n\triangleq\forall\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,\forall h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}},|h({pkt_{in}})|+|h(pkt_{out})|\leq n

Figure 22
.
Bound the size of types

We also need a few lemmas about how this interacts with various types

Lemma D.1 (Refinement Bound).

For every
\Gamma
,
x
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
N
, such that
\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq N
,
\Gamma\vdash\{x:\tau\mid\varphi\}\leq N
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma
,
x
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
N
, be given such that
\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq N
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
.
Further, let
h\in\llbracket\{x:\tau\mid\varphi\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, we also know that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq N
gives us that
|h({pkt_{in}})|+|h(pkt_{out})|\leq N
, which is what we want to show.
∎

Lemma D.2 (Bound Constraints).

For every
\Gamma
,
x
,
y
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
\varphi
, such that
\mathsf{heap}
is the only free variable in
\varphi
,

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N
, if and only if

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma
,
x
,
y
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
\varphi
be given.
Prove each direction separately.

(\Rightarrow)

Assume

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:{\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]
for some
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma
, and some
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
, such that
\llbracket\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}=\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[\mathsf{heap}\mapsto h_{1}]}=\mathit{true}
.
Since
y
does not occur in
\varphi
, then we also have

\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[\mathsf{heap}\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.

Now, consider
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
To show that
|h_{2}({pkt_{in}})|+|h_{2}(pkt_{out})|\leq N
.

Since
\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[\mathsf{heap}\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}=\llbracket\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{2}]}
, we can conclude that
h_{2}\in\llbracket{\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
.
Now, since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})
, the result follows by our initial assumption
assumption
\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})\vdash\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N
.

(\Leftarrow)

Assume
\Gamma,(x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau_{1})
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma
.

Now consider
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
To show
\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert<N
.

By the semantics of heap types, we have
\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[\mathsf{heap}\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.
Since
y
is not free in
\varphi
, we also have
\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[\mathsf{heap}\mapsto h_{1}]}=\llbracket\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}=\mathit{true}
, so we can conclude that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{1}\mid e[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
By our initial assumption, every heap in
\tau_{2}
is bounded and as such also heap
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau_{2}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.

∎

Lemma D.3 (Bound Choice).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}\leq M
, then
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\leq\max(M,N)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We have to show that
\lvert h({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq\max(M,N)
.

Case

M=N
::

Assume
M=N
, so
\max(M,N)=M=N
.
By the semantics of heap types
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
or
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

The result immediately follows by assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

The result immediately follows by assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}\leq M
.

Case

M>N
::

Without loss of generality, we assume that
M>N
, so
\max(M,N)=M
.
By the semantics of heap types
h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
or
h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

By assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
and since by assumption
N<M
, it follows
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq M
.

Subcase

h\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
::

The result immediately follows by assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}\leq M
.

∎

Lemma D.4 (Bound Substitution).

For all
\Gamma
,
y
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
,
N
,

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}[y\mapsto\tau_{1}]\leq N
if and only if

\Gamma,(y:\tau_{1})\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma
,
y
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
N
be given.
Prove each direction separately:

(
\Rightarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}[y\mapsto\tau_{2}]\leq N
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,y:\tau_{1}
such that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
This means there is some
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\models\Gamma
and
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}

such that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
By the semantics of heap types follows that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}[y\mapsto\tau_{1}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
With the initial assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}[y\mapsto\tau_{1}]\leq N
, we can conclude that
\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq N
.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Assume
\Gamma,y:\tau_{1}\models\tau_{2}\leq N
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}[y\mapsto\tau_{1}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, there is some
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
such that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Notice that
\mathcal{E}[y\mapsto h_{1}]\models\Gamma,y:\tau_{1}
.
The initial assumption proves that
|h({pkt_{in}})|+|h(pkt_{out})|\leq N
.

∎

We can then formulate the following two theorems.

Theorem D.5 (Forwards MTU Bound).

For every
\Gamma
,
c
,
x
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, if
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
and
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}
and every ascribed type in
c
is also bounded by
N
, then

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c)

Proof.

Proceed by induction on
c
, leaving
\Gamma
and
N
general.
We refer to the general assumptions as follows:

(A)

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N

(B)

\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}

Case

\mathit{extract(\iota)}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\mathit{extract(\iota)}
is
T-Extract-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid\varphi_{1}\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau_{1},\iota,y)\mid\varphi_{2}\}

(A2):

\varphi_{1}=z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle

(A3):

\varphi_{2}=y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x
.

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{extract(\iota)})=0
, it suffices to show
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By definition of the semantics of heap types we know there are some
h_{21}
and
h_{22}
such that

(A4):

h_{2}=h_{21}+\!\!+\ h_{22}
,

(A5):

h_{21}({pkt_{in}})=h_{21}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle

(A6):

h_{1}({pkt_{in}})=h_{21}(\iota)@h_{22}({pkt_{in}})

(A7):

h_{1}(pkt_{out})=h_{22}(pkt_{out})

We can further conclude that

(A8):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})
,

(A9):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{22}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})[\lvert\iota\rvert\!:\!\ ]

From assumption
(A)
follows

(A10):

\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{1}(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq N

Together with
(A8)
and
(A9)
, we can conclude that
\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert<\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{1}(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq N
.

Case

\mathit{add}
\iota
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\mathit{add}
\iota
is
T-Add-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}{\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.\iota=v\}}.

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{add}{\iota})=0
, it suffices to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
, and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By definition of the semantics of heap types we know there are some
h_{21}
and
h_{22}
such that

(A2):

h_{2}=h_{21}+\!\!+\ h_{22}

(A3):

h_{21}=h_{1}

(A4):

h_{22}({pkt_{in}})=h_{22}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle

From these three equations we can conclude that

(A5):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

(A6):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})

The result follows by assumption
(A)
.

Case

\iota.f:=e
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\iota.f:=e
is
T-Mod-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\{y:\top\mid\varphi_{pkt}\wedge\varphi_{\iota}\wedge\varphi_{f}\wedge y.\iota.f=e[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

(A2):

\varphi_{pkt}=y.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge y.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\iota.f:=e)=0
, it suffices to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
From assumption
(A1)
and by the semantics of heap types follows

(A3):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

(A4):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})

The result follows by assumption
(A)
.

Case

\mathit{remit(\iota)}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\mathit{remit(\iota)}
is
T-Remit-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\tau_{1}\mid z\equiv x\}.\{z:\epsilon\mid\varphi\}

(A2):

\varphi=z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.\iota

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{remit(\iota)})=\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N+\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, there exists
h_{21}
and
h_{22}
such that

(A3):

h_{2}=h_{21}+\!\!+\ h_{22}

(A4):

h_{21}=h_{1}

(A5):

h_{22}({pkt_{in}})=\langle\rangle

(A6):

h_{22}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(\iota)

From
(A4)
and
(A5)
, we can conclude that

(A7):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

From
(A4)
and
(A6)
, we can further conclude that

(A8):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})@h_{1}(\iota)

From
(A7)
and
(A8)
then follows

(A9):

\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert=\lvert h_{1}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{1}(pkt_{out})\rvert+\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)

Together with assumption
(A)
, we can conclude that
\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq N+\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
.

Case

\mathit{reset}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\mathit{reset}
is
T-Reset-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\Sigma y\!:\{z:\epsilon\mid\varphi_{1}\}.\{z:\epsilon\mid\varphi_{2}\}

(A2):

\varphi_{1}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.{pkt_{in}}=x.pkt_{out}

(A3):

\varphi_{2}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\wedge z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{reset})=0
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, there exists
h_{21}
and
h_{22}
such that

(A4):

h_{2}=h_{21}+\!\!+\ h_{22}

(A5):

h_{21}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle

(A6):

h_{21}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})

(A7):

h_{22}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle

(A8):

h_{22}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

By
(A4)
,
(A5)
and
(A7)
follows

(A9):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle

and by
(A4)
,
(A6)
and
(A8)
follows

(A10):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})@h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

Since by assumption
(A)
,
|h_{1}({pkt_{in}})|+|h_{1}(pkt_{out})|\leq N
, by
(A9)
and
(A10)
,
\lvert h_{2}({pkt_{in}})\rvert+\lvert h_{2}(pkt_{out})\rvert\leq N
.

Case

\mathit{if(\varphi)\ then\ c_{1}\ else\ c_{2}}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies is
T-If-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\{y:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}

(A3):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{if(\varphi)\ then\ c_{1}\ else\ c_{2}})=\max(\mathsf{emit}(c_{1}),\mathsf{emit}(c_{2}))
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N+\max(\mathsf{emit}(c_{1}),\mathsf{emit}(c_{2}))
.

By
Lemma

D.1
and assumption
(A)
follows

(A4):

\Gamma\vdash\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N

(A5):

\Gamma\vdash\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N

Applying the IH to
(A2)
and
(A2)
with
(A4)
and
(A5)
respectively, gives

(A6):

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{12}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})

(A7):

\Gamma,x:\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}\vdash\tau_{22}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})

By
Lemma

D.2
with
(A6)
and
(A7)
respectively follows

(A8):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})

(A9):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{22}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})

By
Lemma

D.3
with
(A8)
and
(A9)
follows

(A10):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\{y:\tau_{12}\mid\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}+\{y:\tau_{22}\mid\neg\varphi[x/\mathsf{heap}]\}\leq\max(N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1}),N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2}))

The result follows together with the fact that
\max(A+B,A+C)=A+\max(B,C)
.

Case

c_{1};c_{2}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
c_{1};c_{2}
is
T-Seq-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

(A3):

\tau_{2}=\tau_{22}[y\mapsto\tau_{12}]

Since
\mathsf{emit}(c_{1};c_{2})=\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.

By applying the IH to
(A1)
, we get

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})

Since we left
\Gamma
and
N
general, we can apply the IH again to
(A2)
and get

(A5):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1},y:\tau_{12}\vdash\tau_{22}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})

The result follows by
Lemma

D.4
with
(A5)
.

Case

\mathit{skip}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies to
\mathit{skip}
is
T-Skip-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\tau_{2}=\{y:\tau_{1}\mid y\equiv x\}

Since
\mathsf{emit}(\mathit{skip})=0
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
We can write
\mathcal{E}
as
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
where
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

By the semantics of heap types, follows

(A2):

h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})

(A3):

h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})

The result follows by assumption
(A)
.

Case

c_{0}\ \mathit{as}\ (x:\hat{\tau}_{1})\to\tau_{2}
::

The only algorithmic typing rule that applies is
T-Ascribe-Algo
.
By inversion, we know

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash c_{0}:(x:\hat{\tau}_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{c}

(A2):

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\hat{\tau}_{1}

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\hat{\tau}_{1}\vdash\tau_{c}<:\tau_{2}

Since
\mathsf{emit}(c_{0}\ \mathit{as}\ \sigma)=\mathsf{emit}(c_{0})
, we have to show that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{0})
.
By our initial assumption, every ascribed type is also bounded by
N
.
We therefore have
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}\leq N
from which the result immediately follows.

∎

Theorem D.6 (Decidability).

If
\Gamma
,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
and every ascribed type in
c
are bounded by the MTU
N
,
then
\Gamma\vdash c\!:\!(x:\tau_{1})\to\tau_{2}
is decidable.

Proof.

By
Theorem

C.19
(Algorithmic Typing Correctness), we can equivalently show that
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
are decidable.
By
Theorem

D.5
,
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
is bounded.

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{2}^{\prime}<:\tau_{2}
is therefore decidable by finite enumeration.

To show that
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
is decidable, we proceed by induction on the algorithmic typing derivation.

Case

T-Skip-Algo
::

Immediate, because
T-Skip-Algo
does not perform any subtyping checks.

Case

T-Reset-Algo
::

Also immediate, because
T-Reset-Algo
does not perform any subtyping checks.

Case

T-Seq-Algo
::

By inversion of
T-Seq-Algo
,

(A1):

\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}

(A2):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}

By
(A1)
and
Theorem

D.5
,

(A3):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash\tau_{12}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})

Applying the IH to
(A1)
with assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
and
(A3)
gives us that
\Gamma\vdash c_{1}:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{12}
is decidable.

Again, by
Theorem

D.5
with
(A2)
and
(A3)
, follows

(A4):

\Gamma,x:\tau_{1},y:\tau_{12}\vdash\tau_{22}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{1})+\mathsf{emit}(c_{2})

By IH follows that
\Gamma,x:\tau_{1}\vdash c_{2}:(y:\tau_{12})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{22}
is decidable and thus typechecking the sequence of both commands is decidable.

Case

T-Add-Algo
::

By inversion, we know that
T-Add-Algo
performs the subtyping check
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}
.
To show that typechecking is decidable in this case, we must show that
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\neg x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}
is decidable.
This is the case because we can finitely enumerate the heaps
h
described by
\tau_{1}
and check wether every
h
is a member of
\{x:\top\mid\neg y.\iota.\mathit{valid}\}
.

Case

T-Extract-Algo
::

By inversion, we know that
T-Extract-Algo
performs the subtyping check
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}
.
To show that typechecking is decidable in this case, we must show that
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}
is decidable.
This is the case because we can finitely enumerate the heaps
h
described by
\tau_{1}
and check wether every
h
is a member of
\{x:\top\mid\lvert x.{pkt_{in}}\rvert\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\}
.

Case

T-Remit-Algo
::

Identical to the previous subcase.

Case

T-Mod-Algo
::

Identical to the previous subcase.

Case

T-If-Algo
::

Since
\tau_{1}
is bounded by assumption and refining the input type does not increase the size,
\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}
and
\{y:\tau_{1}\mid\neg\varphi[y/\mathsf{heap}]\}
are still bounded.
By
Theorem

D.5
then follows that the output types of
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are also bounded.
By IH applied to
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
, we get that the algorithmic type checking applied to
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
respectively is decidable and thus checking the conditional is decidable.

Case

T-Ascribe-Algo
::

By assumption,
\Gamma,\tau_{1}
and
\hat{\tau}_{1}
are bounded, so
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\hat{\tau}_{1}
is decidable by finite enumeration.
Since by assumption
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\leq N
, by
Theorem

D.5
follows that
\Gamma,x:\hat{\tau}_{1}\vdash\tau_{c}\leq N+\mathsf{emit}(c_{0})
.
By IH then follows that
\Gamma\vdash c:(x:\tau_{1})\rightsquigarrow\tau_{2}^{\prime}
is decidable.
Since
\tau_{c}
is bounded and by assumption also
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
is bounded, we can finitely enumerate, so
\Gamma,x:\hat{\tau}_{1}\vdash\tau_{c}<:\tau_{2}^{\prime}
is also decidable and thus typechecking an ascribed command is decidable.

∎

Appendix E
Rewrite Optimization Correctness

Definition E.1 (Type Equivalence).

We write
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\doteq\tau_{2}
for the equivalence of types
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
in context
\Gamma
, i.e.,
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\doteq\tau_{2}\triangleq\forall\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma.\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}=\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}

Lemma E.2 (Rewriting Sigma Types).

In any context
\Gamma
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\Gamma\vdash\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\\
\doteq\\
\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\{y:\top\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}y.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}@r.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge\\
y.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}@r.pkt_{out}\end{array}\right)\ \wedge\\
\bigwedge_{\iota\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}y.\iota.\mathit{valid}=x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\oplus r.\iota.\mathit{valid}\ \wedge\\
x.\iota.\mathit{valid}\implies y.\iota=x.\iota\ \wedge\\
r.\iota.\mathit{valid}\implies y.\iota=r.\iota\end{array}\right)\end{array}\right\}\left[r\mapsto\tau_{2}\right][x\mapsto\tau_{1}]\end{array}\end{array}

Proof.

Proof each direction separately.

(
\Rightarrow)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
and let
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, we know there exists
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
and
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.

By definition of heap concatenation,
h({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})@h_{2}({pkt_{in}})
and also
h(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})@h_{2}(pkt_{out})
.
Further,
\mathit{dom}(h)
is the disjoint union of
\mathit{dom}(h_{1})
and
\mathit{dom}(h_{2})
such that if
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(h_{i})
,
h(\iota)=h_{i}(\iota)
for each
i=1,2
and each
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})
.
The result follows by definition of the semantics.

(
\Leftarrow)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma
.
By the definition of the semantics, it suffices to show, for
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
, and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
, and
h\in\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1},r\mapsto h_{2}]}
such that the above refinement holds for
h
, that
h\in\llbracket\Sigma x\!:\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
.
By the semantics of heap types, it suffices to show that
h=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
.
The refinement tells us that

•

h({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}({pkt_{in}})@h_{2}({pkt_{in}})
and

•

h(pkt_{out})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})@h_{2}(pkt_{out})
.

Further,
\mathit{dom}(h)
is the disjoint union of
\mathit{dom}(h_{1})
and
\mathit{dom}(h_{2})
such that if
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(h_{i})
,
h(\iota)=h_{i}(\iota)
for each
i=1,2
and each
\iota\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})
.

∎

Lemma E.3 (Rewriting Refinement Types).

For
\Gamma
,
\tau
,
\iota
,
x
,
y
, such that
x
and
y

do not occur free in
\tau
,

\Gamma,(x:\tau)\vdash\{y:\tau\mid x\equiv y\}\doteq\{y:\top\mid x\equiv y\}

Proof.

Prove each direction separately.

(
\Rightarrow
)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau)
.
We know
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
such that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
Let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau\mid x\equiv y\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Then
h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
and
\llbracket x\equiv y\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.
From the latter, we can conclude that
h_{2}=h_{1}
.
To show
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\top\mid y\equiv x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
, we have to show that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
, which is immediate, and
\llbracket x\equiv y\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
, which immediately follows by the fact that
h_{2}=h_{1}
.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,(x:\tau)
.
We know
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]
such that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
Let
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\top\mid x\equiv y\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
.
Then
h_{2}\in\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
,
and
\llbracket x\equiv y\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.
Observe that
h_{1}=h_{2}
.
To show that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{y:\tau\mid y\equiv x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
, we must show that

h_{2}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h_{1}]}
and
\llbracket x\equiv y\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}[x\mapsto h_{1},y\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
.
The first follows by assumption that
h_{1}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
and the fact that
h_{2}=h_{1}
.
The second immediately follows from
h_{2}=h_{1}
.

∎

Lemma E.4.

For all
\Gamma
,
x
,
\tau
and
\iota
, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{sizeof}_{{pkt_{in}}}(\tau)\geq\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)
and
x
does not occur free in
\tau
, then

\begin{array}[]{c}\Gamma,x:\tau\vdash\\
\text{\Large$\Sigma$}y:\left\{z:\iota\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[center]{l}z.{pkt_{in}}=\langle\rangle\ \wedge\\
z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\end{array}\right\}\mathrel{.}\left\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[center]{l}y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\ \wedge\\
z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\ \wedge\\
z\equiv_{\iota}x\end{array}\right\}\\
\doteq\\
\left\{y:\top\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{l}y.\iota.\mathit{valid}\wedge\bigwedge_{\kappa\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})\wedge\kappa\neq\iota}y.\kappa=x.\kappa\ \wedge\\
y.\iota@y.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge y.pkt_{out}\ =x.pkt_{out}\wedge\end{array}\right\}\end{array}

[Rewrite Sigma Extract]

Proof.

Proof each direction separately.

(
\Rightarrow
)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau
.
We know
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]
such that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
Let
h_{\Sigma}\in\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
be arbitrary.
By the semantics of heap types follows

(A1)

h_{\Sigma}=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}

(A2)

h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}

(A3)

h_{2}\in\llbracket\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1}]}

(A4)

h_{2}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1}]}

(A5)

\llbracket y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1},z\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}

By
Lemma

B.23
, there exists
\hat{h}_{2}\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
such that

(A6)

h_{2}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))

Together with
0(A5)
, we can conclude that

(A7)

\hat{h}_{2}(pkt_{out})=h(pkt_{out})

(A8)

\hat{h}_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})[\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\!:\!\ ]

(A9)

\forall\kappa\neq\iota.\hat{h}_{2}(\kappa)=h(\kappa)

(A10)

\iota\not\in\mathit{dom}(\hat{h}_{2})

h_{\Sigma}\in\llbracket\{y:\top\mid y.\iota.\mathit{valid}\wedge\bigwedge_{\kappa\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})\wedge\kappa\neq\iota}y.\kappa=x.\kappa\ \wedge y.\iota@y.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge y.pkt_{out}\ =x.pkt_{out}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}}
follows by the semantics of heap types with
0(A1)
,
0(A6)
,
0(A7)
,
0(A8)
,
0(A9)
and
0(A10)
.

(
\Leftarrow
)

Let
\mathcal{E}\models\Gamma,x:\tau
.
We know
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]
such that
h\in\llbracket\tau\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.
Let
\hat{h}\in\llbracket\{y:\top\mid y.\iota.\mathit{valid}\wedge\bigwedge_{\kappa\in\mathit{dom}(\mathcal{HT})\wedge\kappa\neq\iota}y.\kappa=x.\kappa\wedge y.\iota@y.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge y.pkt_{out}\ =x.pkt_{out}\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}

By the semantics of heap types,

(A1)

\hat{h}(\iota)=h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]

(A2)

\forall\kappa\neq\iota.\hat{h}(\kappa)=h(\kappa)

(A3)

\hat{h}(pkt_{out})=h(pkt_{out})

(A4)

\hat{h}(\iota)@\hat{h}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})\Leftrightarrow\hat{h}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})[\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)\!:\!\ ]

To show that
\hat{h}\in\llbracket\Sigma y\!:\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}.\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
, we have to show that there exists
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
\hat{h}=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
and
h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
and
h_{2}\in\llbracket\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.

Let
h_{1}({pkt_{in}})=h_{1}(pkt_{out})=\langle\rangle
and
h_{1}(\iota)=h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]
and no other instances be valid in heap
h_{1}
.

h_{1}\in\llbracket\{z:\iota\mid z.{pkt_{in}}=z.pkt_{out}=\langle\rangle\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h]}
then follows by the semantics of heap types.
By
Lemma

B.20
, there exists
h_{2}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[y\mapsto h_{1}]}
such that
h_{2}=\mathsf{chomp}^{\Downarrow}(h,\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota))
.
Since
x
not free in
\tau
, it also holds that
h_{2}\in\llbracket\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.

Since
h_{1}(\iota)@h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h({pkt_{in}})
,
h_{2}(pkt_{out})=h(pkt_{out})
and since
\mathsf{chomp}
does not change already valid header instances also for all
\kappa\neq\iota
,
h_{2}(\kappa)=h(\kappa)
, we can conclude that

\llbracket y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1},z\mapsto h_{2}]}=\mathit{true}
and thus

h_{2}\in\llbracket\{z:\mathsf{chomp}(\tau,\iota,y)\mid y.\iota@z.{pkt_{in}}=x.{pkt_{in}}\wedge z.pkt_{out}=x.pkt_{out}\wedge z\equiv_{\iota}x\}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[x\mapsto h,y\mapsto h_{1}]}
.

By the semantics of heap types, we can further conclude that

(A5)

(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2})({pkt_{in}})=h_{2}({pkt_{in}})=h^{\prime}({pkt_{in}})

(A6)

(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2})(pkt_{out})=h(pkt_{out})=h^{\prime}(pkt_{out})

(A7)

(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2})(\iota)=h({pkt_{in}})[0\!:\!\mathsf{sizeof}(\iota)]=h^{\prime}(\iota)

(A8)

\forall\kappa\neq\iota.(h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2})(\kappa)=h_{2}(\kappa)=h(\kappa)=h^{\prime}(\kappa)

This show that actually
h^{\prime}=h_{1}+\!\!+\ h_{2}
and concludes this case.

∎
